> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy

        Idea:
Implement the smallest concrete build recommendation in docs/runpod_template_runner.md: lift the existing ad-hoc RunPod glue out of scripts/ into a clean importable vibecomfy.runpod package, so any caller (tests, CLI, future agents) can spin up a pod and run a ready template end-to-end with one async call.

This is a follow-up to the in-flight P1 megaplan (p1-foundation-typed-handle-20260426-1927). Zero file overlap with P1 — P1 owns vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, and tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. This megaplan owns vibecomfy/runpod/, scripts/runpod_runner.py (refactor), vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py (touch only if needed), and pyproject.toml.

DELIVERABLES — all must land:

1. New package vibecomfy/runpod/ with at least:
   - runner.py exposing RunPodTemplate with the async-context-manager API shown in the doc:
     async with RunPodTemplate.launch('image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i', gpu='RTX 4090') as result:
         assert result.status == 'ok'
         assert result.media_files
         # result.elapsed, result.logs, result.artifact_dir
   - result.py (or co-located) defining a RunResult dataclass with at minimum: status (Literal['ok','error','timeout']), media_files (list[Path]), elapsed (float seconds), logs (str or Path), artifact_dir (Path), pod_id (str), template_id (str). Add error_message field for non-ok statuses.
   - __init__.py exporting RunPodTemplate and RunResult.

2. Lift PodGuard and lifecycle-import helper out of scripts/runpod_runner.py (current locations: PodGuard at lines 27-82, _runpod_lifecycle() at lines 264-271) into vibecomfy/runpod/. Keep scripts/runpod_runner.py callers working via re-export — the script layer should import from the new package and re-expose the same names so existing callers in scripts/ and any external users of these scripts don't break.

3. Small remote script sent by RunPodTemplate.launch(...) that does: install vibecomfy on the pod → load template via vibecomfy.registry.ready.workflow_from_ready(template_id) → vibecomfy validate → vibecomfy run → emit a JSON result blob with media paths, timings, status, error message if any. NOT the 750-line bash blob from scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py:_remote_script — keep this one narrow (target: under 100 lines of remote bash/python). The RunPodTemplate launch flow parses that JSON into RunResult.

4. Declare runpod-lifecycle as a real dependency in pyproject.toml. Today it's discovered via sys.path / env-var hacks (VIBECOMFY_RUNPOD_LIFECYCLE_ROOT, sibling ../runpod-lifecycle checkout). Use [tool.uv.sources] with a path dep, OR optional-dependency, OR whichever pattern matches the project's current dependency management style — read pyproject.toml first to match conventions. Preserve the env-var fallback for now (don't break existing dev setups), but the canonical path becomes the declared dep.

5. One @pytest.mark.gpu test that exercises Flux Klein 4B T2I end-to-end against a live pod via RunPodTemplate.launch('image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i', gpu='RTX 4090'). Asserts result.status == 'ok', result.media_files non-empty, timings within reasonable bound. Marked opt-in — never runs in default pytest, only with pytest -m gpu or equivalent. DO NOT EXECUTE THIS TEST during the megaplan run — provisioning real GPU pods burns credentials/budget. The deliverable is the test file existing and being correctly marked; the user runs it post-merge.

CRITICAL CONSTRAINTS:

- Backward compatibility: existing scripts/runpod_runner.py callers (and scripts/runpod_validate.py, scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py if they import from it) must keep working. Re-export PodGuard, run_pod, run_pod_detached, _runpod_lifecycle from the script-level module so import paths stay valid.
- Do NOT touch P1 territory: vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. P1 megaplan is in flight.
- Do NOT execute the GPU test as part of this megaplan — only scaffold it.
- Do NOT rewrite the corpus matrix (scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py). The doc notes that's a future follow-up 'once that proves out.' Out of scope.

KEY FILES (read first):

- docs/runpod_template_runner.md — full survey + recommendation (the spec for this work).
- scripts/runpod_runner.py — PodGuard (27-82), run_pod (120-163), run_pod_detached (166-261), _runpod_lifecycle (264-271). Source for the lift.
- vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py (10-43) — existing CLI bridge using the same sibling/env-var trick.
- vibecomfy/registry/ready.py — ready_template_ids(), workflow_from_ready().
- tests/test_ready_templates.py (27-33) — local validation pattern.
- scripts/runpod_validate.py (1-37) — minimal smoke runner pattern.
- scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py:_remote_script (43-766) — the 750-line bash blob the new wrapper must NOT recreate; reference for what the 'minimal' version needs to do.
- pyproject.toml — declare runpod-lifecycle dep here.
- docs/runpod.md — existing operator doc; check if it needs a pointer to the new module.

ACCEPTANCE:

- All existing tests pass unchanged.
- from vibecomfy.runpod import RunPodTemplate, RunResult succeeds in a clean Python session.
- RunPodTemplate.launch(template_id, gpu=...) returns an async context manager that yields a RunResult typed correctly.
- scripts/runpod_runner.py imports continue to work via re-export.
- runpod-lifecycle is declared in pyproject.toml.
- pytest --collect-only -m gpu lists the new Flux Klein 4B T2I test.
- The new wrapper's remote script is documented (docstring or short doc note) and is materially shorter than the corpus-matrix bash blob.

  "meta_commentary": "Execution order is critical: T1\u2192T2\u2192T3 (lift) \u2192 T4 (re-export) \u2192 T5/T6 (data + remote script, independent) \u2192 T7 (runner) \u2192 T8 (__init__) \u2192 T9 (pyproject) \u2192 T10/T11/T12 (test scaffold + docs + cleanup test) \u2192 T13 (validation). T1, T3, T5, T6, T9 have no inter-dependencies and can be implemented in parallel if the executor wants speed.\n\nThree structural fixes from the v1\u2192v2 critique are load-bearing \u2014 do not regress them:\n\n1. **FLAG-001 (cycle):** the upload helpers MUST live in vibecomfy/runpod/upload.py, not stay in scripts/runpod_runner.py. If you let the runner import upload helpers from scripts/, package init creates a cycle (scripts \u2192 vibecomfy.runpod.pod_guard \u2192 re-enters package init while __init__.py is mid-execution). Step 14.1's grep audit is the enforcement mechanism.\n\n2. **FLAG-002 (remote install):** the remote pod's bash script installs `.[dev]` ONLY. It must NOT try to install `.[runpod-launch]` or `.[runpod-local]`. Why: runpod-lifecycle is declared via `[tool.uv.sources]` pointing at a sibling checkout that the pod upload doesn't include. Plain pip on the pod can't resolve `[tool.uv.sources]` and would crash before validation. The pod is the *target* of launches \u2014 it doesn't need to launch pods itself.\n\n3. **FLAG-003 (cleanup):** RunPodTemplate.launch MUST be `@asynccontextmanager`, not a class with `__aenter__`/`__aexit__`. Python skips `__aexit__` if `__aenter__` raises \u2014 that would leak live pods. The generator-based form puts try/finally directly around `yield`, so terminate() runs no matter where the failure happens. T12's monkeypatch test is the objective evidence.\n\nLazy lifecycle import is the fourth invariant: nothing in vibecomfy/runpod/ does a top-level `import runpod_lifecycle`. Only `_runpod_lifecycle()` body has it, only `PodGuard.launch()` calls it. This is what lets a clean .[dev]-only install successfully `from vibecomfy.runpod import RunPodTemplate, RunResult` \u2014 verified by Step 12.1 / SC8.\n\nFor T7 specifically: the existing scripts/runpod_runner.py:130-163 has the same try/finally cleanup pattern around run_pod's body \u2014 mirror that approach. Use the existing exclude-set conventions from scripts/runpod_runner.py (look at how run_pod calls upload_dir for the default ignore patterns).\n\nFor T6: keep the inline Python tight. Let it call `vibecomfy.registry.ready.workflow_from_ready(template_id)` then subprocess `python -m vibecomfy.cli run` (matches the already-exercised path in scripts/runpod_validate.py:22-26). Don't reimplement workflow execution inline \u2014 that's how you get back to a 750-line bash blob.\n\nFor T9: read pyproject.toml first to see the existing optional-dependencies layout. Follow whatever indentation/quote style is already in use. The `[tool.uv.sources]` block goes near the bottom (or wherever uv config conventions place it for this project).\n\nFor T13: the GPU test MUST NOT execute. `pytest --collect-only -m gpu` is collection only \u2014 safe. Real `pytest -m gpu` provisions a real pod and burns budget. If a validation step would require provisioning, skip it.\n\nP1 boundary is non-negotiable: do not touch vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, or tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. Scaffolding the GPU test under tests/gpu/ (not tests/smoke/) is part of this \u2014 different directory than P1's smoke tests.\n\nTwo open questions inherited from the plan that the executor can decide pragmatically:\n- vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py duplication: leave untouched (brief said 'touch only if needed').\n- runpod-launch naming: keep `runpod-launch` as the new extra (do not rename/delete runpod-local \u2014 preserves backward compat for any user who installs it today).\n\nBaseline tests not captured (test command was not approved for execution before plan finalization). Executor should run `pytest -q` early to capture pre-existing failures and distinguish them from regressions introduced by this work.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan places _coercion_log.py under vibecomfy/blocks/ but also needs vibeworkflow.connect in vibecomfy/workflow.py to consult handle_trace_active and record events. because importing vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log executes vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py, and that package currently imports vibeworkflow at top level, any top-level import from workflow.py to vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log would create a circular import risk unless the implementation uses carefully lazy imports or moves the shared trace module outside the blocks package. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the proposed _coercion_log.py under vibecomfy/blocks/ is supporting infrastructure that both blocks and workflow need. given the current package dependency direction (blocks imports workflow), the safer location is a neutral module such as vibecomfy/handles.py or vibecomfy/handle_warnings.py; leaving it under blocks increases import-order fragility unless the implementation is very deliberate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: for the block helper caller path, vibeworkflow.connect is usually called by vibecomfy.blocks._utils.connect, not by the user's block function directly. the plan's immediate-caller-frame heuristic can work if _utils.connect has a handle local named source, but it will not identify the higher-level block function unless record_raw_ref_coercion preserves or walks to the block frame rather than skipping blocks frames. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handle-equality-hash-semantics: strict python hash/eq invariant violated for the bare-id compatibility bridge (handle('1', 0) == '1' and == '1.0' but hash('1') != hash('1.0')). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handle-equality-hash-semantics: hash/eq compromise is documented but not strictly satisfied for bare-id bridge. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] import-architecture: import architecture: placing shared trace state in `vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log` risks circular imports because `workflow.py` needs the state for connect-site detection while `vibecomfy.blocks.__init__` currently imports `vibeworkflow` at package import time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the plan's record_raw_ref_coercion helper says it finds the first non-vibecomfy/blocks frame for the site. that conflicts with the stated goal that raw string refs in block code are visible: a missed handles(image="...") inside a block called by a ready template could be attributed to an allowlisted ready_templates frame and suppressed, or at minimum would not identify the block module as the source. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] untyped-raw-ref-doctor-rule-coverage: runtime detection covers handles values and connect refs but not arbitrary python dict keys outside those flows. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] wf-node-out-api-contract: non-digit slot names raise notimplementederror instead of returning a handle; diverges from brief's .out(name_or_index) literal wording. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] wf-node-out-api-contract: same as issue_hints-1. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

The payload schema is\n+``{status, template_id, pod_id, elapsed_seconds, media_files, error_message}``.\n+The rendered bash is intentionally kept under 100 lines so it remains a narrow\n+single-template runner, not a replacement for the corpus matrix harness.\n+\"\"\"\n+\n+\n+def build_remote_script(template_id: str, *, remote_root: str, payload_path: str, output_dir: str) -> str:\n+    template = shlex.quote(template_id)\n+    root = shlex.quote(remote_root)\n+    payload = shlex.quote(payload_path)\n+    output = shlex.quote(output_dir)\n+    return f\"\"\"#!/usr/bin/env bash\n+set -e\n+PAYLOAD={payload}\n+write_fallback() {{\n+  if [ ! -s \"$PAYLOAD\" ]; then\n+    python3 - \"$PAYLOAD\" {template} <<'PY'\n+import json, os, sys, time\n+json.dump({{\"status\":\"error\",\"template_id\":sys.argv[2],\"pod_id\":os.getenv(\"RUNPOD_POD_ID\",\"\"),\"elapsed_seconds\":0.0,\"media_files\":[],\"error_message\":\"remote runner exited before writing payload\"}}, open(sys.argv[1], \"w\"))\n+PY\n+  fi\n+}}\n+trap write_fallback EXIT\n+cd {root}\n+python3 -m pip install -e '.[dev]'\n+python3 -m pip install 'comfyui@git+https://github.com/hiddenswitch/ComfyUI.git' 'comfy-script[default]'\n+python3 - \"$PAYLOAD\" {template} {output} <<'PY'\n+import json, os, subprocess, sys, time\n+from pathlib import Path\n+from vibecomfy.registry.ready import workflow_from_ready\n+\n+payload_path, template_id, output_dir = sys.argv[1:4]\n+start = time.time()\n+status = \"ok\"\n+error = None\n+media = []\n+try:\n+    workflow = workflow_from_ready(template_id)\n+    report = workflow.validate()\n+    if not report.ok:\n+        raise RuntimeError(\"; \".join(issue.message for issue in report.issues))\n+    subprocess.run([sys.executable, \"-m\", \"vibecomfy.cli\", \"run\", template_id, \"--ready\"], check=True)\n+    root = Path(output_dir)\n+    suffixes = {{\".png\", \".mp4\", \".webp\", \".webm\", \".wav\"}}\n+    if root.exists():\n+        for path in root.rglob(\"*\"):\n+            if path.is_file() and path.suffix.lower() in suffixes and path.stat().st_mtime >= start:\n+                media.append(path.as_posix())\n+except Exception as exc:\n+    status = \"error\"\n+    error = f\"{{type(exc).__name__}}: {{exc}}\"\n+elapsed = time.time() - start\n+Path(payload_path).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+with open(payload_path, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as handle:\n+    json.dump({{\"status\": status, \"template_id\": template_id, \"pod_id\": os.getenv(\"RUNPOD_POD_ID\", \"\"), \"elapsed_seconds\": elapsed, \"media_files\": media, \"error_message\": error}}, handle)\n+if status != \"ok\":\n+    raise SystemExit(1)\n+PY\n+\"\"\"\n*** End Patch\n"

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy

        Idea:
Implement the smallest concrete build recommendation in docs/runpod_template_runner.md: lift the existing ad-hoc RunPod glue out of scripts/ into a clean importable vibecomfy.runpod package, so any caller (tests, CLI, future agents) can spin up a pod and run a ready template end-to-end with one async call.

This is a follow-up to the in-flight P1 megaplan (p1-foundation-typed-handle-20260426-1927). Zero file overlap with P1 — P1 owns vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, and tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. This megaplan owns vibecomfy/runpod/, scripts/runpod_runner.py (refactor), vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py (touch only if needed), and pyproject.toml.

DELIVERABLES — all must land:

1. New package vibecomfy/runpod/ with at least:
   - runner.py exposing RunPodTemplate with the async-context-manager API shown in the doc:
     async with RunPodTemplate.launch('image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i', gpu='RTX 4090') as result:
         assert result.status == 'ok'
         assert result.media_files
         # result.elapsed, result.logs, result.artifact_dir
   - result.py (or co-located) defining a RunResult dataclass with at minimum: status (Literal['ok','error','timeout']), media_files (list[Path]), elapsed (float seconds), logs (str or Path), artifact_dir (Path), pod_id (str), template_id (str). Add error_message field for non-ok statuses.
   - __init__.py exporting RunPodTemplate and RunResult.

2. Lift PodGuard and lifecycle-import helper out of scripts/runpod_runner.py (current locations: PodGuard at lines 27-82, _runpod_lifecycle() at lines 264-271) into vibecomfy/runpod/. Keep scripts/runpod_runner.py callers working via re-export — the script layer should import from the new package and re-expose the same names so existing callers in scripts/ and any external users of these scripts don't break.

3. Small remote script sent by RunPodTemplate.launch(...) that does: install vibecomfy on the pod → load template via vibecomfy.registry.ready.workflow_from_ready(template_id) → vibecomfy validate → vibecomfy run → emit a JSON result blob with media paths, timings, status, error message if any. NOT the 750-line bash blob from scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py:_remote_script — keep this one narrow (target: under 100 lines of remote bash/python). The RunPodTemplate launch flow parses that JSON into RunResult.

4. Declare runpod-lifecycle as a real dependency in pyproject.toml. Today it's discovered via sys.path / env-var hacks (VIBECOMFY_RUNPOD_LIFECYCLE_ROOT, sibling ../runpod-lifecycle checkout). Use [tool.uv.sources] with a path dep, OR optional-dependency, OR whichever pattern matches the project's current dependency management style — read pyproject.toml first to match conventions. Preserve the env-var fallback for now (don't break existing dev setups), but the canonical path becomes the declared dep.

5. One @pytest.mark.gpu test that exercises Flux Klein 4B T2I end-to-end against a live pod via RunPodTemplate.launch('image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i', gpu='RTX 4090'). Asserts result.status == 'ok', result.media_files non-empty, timings within reasonable bound. Marked opt-in — never runs in default pytest, only with pytest -m gpu or equivalent. DO NOT EXECUTE THIS TEST during the megaplan run — provisioning real GPU pods burns credentials/budget. The deliverable is the test file existing and being correctly marked; the user runs it post-merge.

CRITICAL CONSTRAINTS:

- Backward compatibility: existing scripts/runpod_runner.py callers (and scripts/runpod_validate.py, scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py if they import from it) must keep working. Re-export PodGuard, run_pod, run_pod_detached, _runpod_lifecycle from the script-level module so import paths stay valid.
- Do NOT touch P1 territory: vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. P1 megaplan is in flight.
- Do NOT execute the GPU test as part of this megaplan — only scaffold it.
- Do NOT rewrite the corpus matrix (scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py). The doc notes that's a future follow-up 'once that proves out.' Out of scope.

KEY FILES (read first):

- docs/runpod_template_runner.md — full survey + recommendation (the spec for this work).
- scripts/runpod_runner.py — PodGuard (27-82), run_pod (120-163), run_pod_detached (166-261), _runpod_lifecycle (264-271). Source for the lift.
- vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py (10-43) — existing CLI bridge using the same sibling/env-var trick.
- vibecomfy/registry/ready.py — ready_template_ids(), workflow_from_ready().

  "meta_commentary": "Execution order is critical: T1\u2192T2\u2192T3 (lift) \u2192 T4 (re-export) \u2192 T5/T6 (data + remote script, independent) \u2192 T7 (runner) \u2192 T8 (__init__) \u2192 T9 (pyproject) \u2192 T10/T11/T12 (test scaffold + docs + cleanup test) \u2192 T13 (validation). T1, T3, T5, T6, T9 have no inter-dependencies and can be implemented in parallel if the executor wants speed.\n\nThree structural fixes from the v1\u2192v2 critique are load-bearing \u2014 do not regress them:\n\n1. **FLAG-001 (cycle):** the upload helpers MUST live in vibecomfy/runpod/upload.py, not stay in scripts/runpod_runner.py. If you let the runner import upload helpers from scripts/, package init creates a cycle (scripts \u2192 vibecomfy.runpod.pod_guard \u2192 re-enters package init while __init__.py is mid-execution). Step 14.1's grep audit is the enforcement mechanism.\n\n2. **FLAG-002 (remote install):** the remote pod's bash script installs `.[dev]` ONLY. It must NOT try to install `.[runpod-launch]` or `.[runpod-local]`. Why: runpod-lifecycle is declared via `[tool.uv.sources]` pointing at a sibling checkout that the pod upload doesn't include. Plain pip on the pod can't resolve `[tool.uv.sources]` and would crash before validation. The pod is the *target* of launches \u2014 it doesn't need to launch pods itself.\n\n3. **FLAG-003 (cleanup):** RunPodTemplate.launch MUST be `@asynccontextmanager`, not a class with `__aenter__`/`__aexit__`. Python skips `__aexit__` if `__aenter__` raises \u2014 that would leak live pods. The generator-based form puts try/finally directly around `yield`, so terminate() runs no matter where the failure happens. T12's monkeypatch test is the objective evidence.\n\nLazy lifecycle import is the fourth invariant: nothing in vibecomfy/runpod/ does a top-level `import runpod_lifecycle`. Only `_runpod_lifecycle()` body has it, only `PodGuard.launch()` calls it. This is what lets a clean .[dev]-only install successfully `from vibecomfy.runpod import RunPodTemplate, RunResult` \u2014 verified by Step 12.1 / SC8.\n\nFor T7 specifically: the existing scripts/runpod_runner.py:130-163 has the same try/finally cleanup pattern around run_pod's body \u2014 mirror that approach. Use the existing exclude-set conventions from scripts/runpod_runner.py (look at how run_pod calls upload_dir for the default ignore patterns).\n\nFor T6: keep the inline Python tight. Let it call `vibecomfy.registry.ready.workflow_from_ready(template_id)` then subprocess `python -m vibecomfy.cli run` (matches the already-exercised path in scripts/runpod_validate.py:22-26). Don't reimplement workflow execution inline \u2014 that's how you get back to a 750-line bash blob.\n\nFor T9: read pyproject.toml first to see the existing optional-dependencies layout. Follow whatever indentation/quote style is already in use. The `[tool.uv.sources]` block goes near the bottom (or wherever uv config conventions place it for this project).\n\nFor T13: the GPU test MUST NOT execute. `pytest --collect-only -m gpu` is collection only \u2014 safe. Real `pytest -m gpu` provisions a real pod and burns budget. If a validation step would require provisioning, skip it.\n\nP1 boundary is non-negotiable: do not touch vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, or tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. Scaffolding the GPU test under tests/gpu/ (not tests/smoke/) is part of this \u2014 different directory than P1's smoke tests.\n\nTwo open questions inherited from the plan that the executor can decide pragmatically:\n- vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py duplication: leave untouched (brief said 'touch only if needed').\n- runpod-launch naming: keep `runpod-launch` as the new extra (do not rename/delete runpod-local \u2014 preserves backward compat for any user who installs it today).\n\nBaseline tests not captured (test command was not approved for execution before plan finalization). Executor should run `pytest -q` early to capture pre-existing failures and distinguish them from regressions introduced by this work.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan places _coercion_log.py under vibecomfy/blocks/ but also needs vibeworkflow.connect in vibecomfy/workflow.py to consult handle_trace_active and record events. because importing vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log executes vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py, and that package currently imports vibeworkflow at top level, any top-level import from workflow.py to vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log would create a circular import risk unless the implementation uses carefully lazy imports or moves the shared trace module outside the blocks package. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the proposed _coercion_log.py under vibecomfy/blocks/ is supporting infrastructure that both blocks and workflow need. given the current package dependency direction (blocks imports workflow), the safer location is a neutral module such as vibecomfy/handles.py or vibecomfy/handle_warnings.py; leaving it under blocks increases import-order fragility unless the implementation is very deliberate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: for the block helper caller path, vibeworkflow.connect is usually called by vibecomfy.blocks._utils.connect, not by the user's block function directly. the plan's immediate-caller-frame heuristic can work if _utils.connect has a handle local named source, but it will not identify the higher-level block function unless record_raw_ref_coercion preserves or walks to the block frame rather than skipping blocks frames. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handle-equality-hash-semantics: strict python hash/eq invariant violated for the bare-id compatibility bridge (handle('1', 0) == '1' and == '1.0' but hash('1') != hash('1.0')). (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handle-equality-hash-semantics: hash/eq compromise is documented but not strictly satisfied for bare-id bridge. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] import-architecture: import architecture: placing shared trace state in `vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log` risks circular imports because `workflow.py` needs the state for connect-site detection while `vibecomfy.blocks.__init__` currently imports `vibeworkflow` at package import time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the plan's record_raw_ref_coercion helper says it finds the first non-vibecomfy/blocks frame for the site. that conflicts with the stated goal that raw string refs in block code are visible: a missed handles(image="...") inside a block called by a ready template could be attributed to an allowlisted ready_templates frame and suppressed, or at minimum would not identify the block module as the source.         Idea:
Implement the smallest concrete build recommendation in docs/runpod_template_runner.md: lift the existing ad-hoc RunPod glue out of scripts/ into a clean importable vibecomfy.runpod package, so any caller (tests, CLI, future agents) can spin up a pod and run a ready template end-to-end with one async call.

This is a follow-up to the in-flight P1 megaplan (p1-foundation-typed-handle-20260426-1927). Zero file overlap with P1 — P1 owns vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, and tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. This megaplan owns vibecomfy/runpod/, scripts/runpod_runner.py (refactor), vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py (touch only if needed), and pyproject.toml.

DELIVERABLES — all must land:

1. New package vibecomfy/runpod/ with at least:
   - runner.py exposing RunPodTemplate with the async-context-manager API shown in the doc:
     async with RunPodTemplate.launch('image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i', gpu='RTX 4090') as result:
         assert result.status == 'ok'
         assert result.media_files
         # result.elapsed, result.logs, result.artifact_dir
   - result.py (or co-located) defining a RunResult dataclass with at minimum: status (Literal['ok','error','timeout']), media_files (list[Path]), elapsed (float seconds), logs (str or Path), artifact_dir (Path), pod_id (str), template_id (str). Add error_message field for non-ok statuses.
   - __init__.py exporting RunPodTemplate and RunResult.

2. Lift PodGuard and lifecycle-import helper out of scripts/runpod_runner.py (current locations: PodGuard at lines 27-82, _runpod_lifecycle() at lines 264-271) into vibecomfy/runpod/. Keep scripts/runpod_runner.py callers working via re-export — the script layer should import from the new package and re-expose the same names so existing callers in scripts/ and any external users of these scripts don't break.

3. Small remote script sent by RunPodTemplate.launch(...) that does: install vibecomfy on the pod → load template via vibecomfy.registry.ready.workflow_from_ready(template_id) → vibecomfy validate → vibecomfy run → emit a JSON result blob with media paths, timings, status, error message if any. NOT the 750-line bash blob from scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py:_remote_script — keep this one narrow (target: under 100 lines of remote bash/python). The RunPodTemplate launch flow parses that JSON into RunResult.

4. Declare runpod-lifecycle as a real dependency in pyproject.toml. Today it's discovered via sys.path / env-var hacks (VIBECOMFY_RUNPOD_LIFECYCLE_ROOT, sibling ../runpod-lifecycle checkout). Use [tool.uv.sources] with a path dep, OR optional-dependency, OR whichever pattern matches the project's current dependency management style — read pyproject.toml first to match conventions. Preserve the env-var fallback for now (don't break existing dev setups), but the canonical path becomes the declared dep.

5. One @pytest.mark.gpu test that exercises Flux Klein 4B T2I end-to-end against a live pod via RunPodTemplate.launch('image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i', gpu='RTX 4090'). Asserts result.status == 'ok', result.media_files non-empty, timings within reasonable bound. Marked opt-in — never runs in default pytest, only with pytest -m gpu or equivalent. DO NOT EXECUTE THIS TEST during the megaplan run — provisioning real GPU pods burns credentials/budget. The deliverable is the test file existing and being correctly marked; the user runs it post-merge.

CRITICAL CONSTRAINTS:

- Backward compatibility: existing scripts/runpod_runner.py callers (and scripts/runpod_validate.py, scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py if they import from it) must keep working. Re-export PodGuard, run_pod, run_pod_detached, _runpod_lifecycle from the script-level module so import paths stay valid.
- Do NOT touch P1 territory: vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. P1 megaplan is in flight.
- Do NOT execute the GPU test as part of this megaplan — only scaffold it.
- Do NOT rewrite the corpus matrix (scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py). The doc notes that's a future follow-up 'once that proves out.' Out of scope.

KEY FILES (read first):

- docs/runpod_template_runner.md — full survey + recommendation (the spec for this work).
- scripts/runpod_runner.py — PodGuard (27-82), run_pod (120-163), run_pod_detached (166-261), _runpod_lifecycle (264-271). Source for the lift.
- vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py (10-43) — existing CLI bridge using the same sibling/env-var trick.
- vibecomfy/registry/ready.py — ready_template_ids(), workflow_from_ready().
- tests/test_ready_templates.py (27-33) — local validation pattern.
- scripts/runpod_validate.py (1-37) — minimal smoke runner pattern.

- pyproject.toml — declare runpod-lifecycle dep here.
- docs/runpod.md — existing operator doc; check if it needs a pointer to the new module.

ACCEPTANCE:

- from vibecomfy.runpod import RunPodTemplate, RunResult succeeds in a clean Python session.
- RunPodTemplate.launch(template_id, gpu=...) returns an async context manager that yields a RunResult typed correctly.

  "meta_commentary": "Execution order is critical: T1\u2192T2\u2192T3 (lift) \u2192 T4 (re-export) \u2192 T5/T6 (data + remote script, independent) \u2192 T7 (runner) \u2192 T8 (__init__) \u2192 T9 (pyproject) \u2192 T10/T11/T12 (test scaffold + docs + cleanup test) \u2192 T13 (validation). T1, T3, T5, T6, T9 have no inter-dependencies and can be implemented in parallel if the executor wants speed.\n\nThree structural fixes from the v1\u2192v2 critique are load-bearing \u2014 do not regress them:\n\n1. **FLAG-001 (cycle):** the upload helpers MUST live in vibecomfy/runpod/upload.py, not stay in scripts/runpod_runner.py. If you let the runner import upload helpers from scripts/, package init creates a cycle (scripts \u2192 vibecomfy.runpod.pod_guard \u2192 re-enters package init while __init__.py is mid-execution). Step 14.1's grep audit is the enforcement mechanism.\n\n2. **FLAG-002 (remote install):** the remote pod's bash script installs `.[dev]` ONLY. It must NOT try to install `.[runpod-launch]` or `.[runpod-local]`. Why: runpod-lifecycle is declared via `[tool.uv.sources]` pointing at a sibling checkout that the pod upload doesn't include. Plain pip on the pod can't resolve `[tool.uv.sources]` and would crash before validation. The pod is the *target* of launches \u2014 it doesn't need to launch pods itself.\n\n3. **FLAG-003 (cleanup):** RunPodTemplate.launch MUST be `@asynccontextmanager`, not a class with `__aenter__`/`__aexit__`. Python skips `__aexit__` if `__aenter__` raises \u2014 that would leak live pods. The generator-based form puts try/finally directly around `yield`, so terminate() runs no matter where the failure happens. T12's monkeypatch test is the objective evidence.\n\nLazy lifecycle import is the fourth invariant: nothing in vibecomfy/runpod/ does a top-level `import runpod_lifecycle`. Only `_runpod_lifecycle()` body has it, only `PodGuard.launch()` calls it. This is what lets a clean .[dev]-only install successfully `from vibecomfy.runpod import RunPodTemplate, RunResult` \u2014 verified by Step 12.1 / SC8.\n\nFor T7 specifically: the existing scripts/runpod_runner.py:130-163 has the same try/finally cleanup pattern around run_pod's body \u2014 mirror that approach. Use the existing exclude-set conventions from scripts/runpod_runner.py (look at how run_pod calls upload_dir for the default ignore patterns).\n\nFor T6: keep the inline Python tight. Let it call `vibecomfy.registry.ready.workflow_from_ready(template_id)` then subprocess `python -m vibecomfy.cli run` (matches the already-exercised path in scripts/runpod_validate.py:22-26). Don't reimplement workflow execution inline \u2014 that's how you get back to a 750-line bash blob.\n\nFor T9: read pyproject.toml first to see the existing optional-dependencies layout. Follow whatever indentation/quote style is already in use. The `[tool.uv.sources]` block goes near the bottom (or wherever uv config conventions place it for this project).\n\nFor T13: the GPU test MUST NOT execute. `pytest --collect-only -m gpu` is collection only \u2014 safe. Real `pytest -m gpu` provisions a real pod and burns budget. If a validation step would require provisioning, skip it.\n\nP1 boundary is non-negotiable: do not touch vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, or tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. Scaffolding the GPU test under tests/gpu/ (not tests/smoke/) is part of this \u2014 different directory than P1's smoke tests.\n\nTwo open questions inherited from the plan that the executor can decide pragmatically:\n- vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py duplication: leave untouched (brief said 'touch only if needed').\n- runpod-launch naming: keep `runpod-launch` as the new extra (do not rename/delete runpod-local \u2014 preserves backward compat for any user who installs it today).\n\nBaseline tests not captured (test command was not approved for execution before plan finalization). Executor should run `pytest -q` early to capture pre-existing failures and distinguish them from regressions introduced by this work.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan places _coercion_log.py under vibecomfy/blocks/ but also needs vibeworkflow.connect in vibecomfy/workflow.py to consult handle_trace_active and record events. because importing vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log executes vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py, and that package currently imports vibeworkflow at top level, any top-level import from workflow.py to vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log would create a circular import risk unless the implementation uses carefully lazy imports or moves the shared trace module outside the blocks package. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the proposed _coercion_log.py under vibecomfy/blocks/ is supporting infrastructure that both blocks and workflow need. given the current package dependency direction (blocks imports workflow), the safer location is a neutral module such as vibecomfy/handles.py or vibecomfy/handle_warnings.py; leaving it under blocks increases import-order fragility unless the implementation is very deliberate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: for the block helper caller path, vibeworkflow.connect is usually called by vibecomfy.blocks._utils.connect, not by the user's block function directly. the plan's immediate-caller-frame heuristic can work if _utils.connect has a handle local named source, but it will not identify the higher-level block function unless record_raw_ref_coercion preserves or walks to the block frame rather than skipping blocks frames.   "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy/vibecomfy/runpod/runner.py\n+from __future__ import annotations\n+\n+import asyncio\n+import json\n+import posixpath\n+import tempfile\n+import time\n+from contextlib import asynccontextmanager\n+from datetime import UTC, datetime\n+from pathlib import Path\n+from typing import AsyncIterator\n+\n+from vibecomfy.runpod._remote import build_remote_script\n+from vibecomfy.runpod.pod_guard import PodGuard\n+from vibecomfy.runpod.result import RunResult\n+from vibecomfy.runpod.upload import upload_dir\n+\n+\n+ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\n+REMOTE_ROOT = \"/workspace/vibecomfy\"\n+REMOTE_PAYLOAD = \"/tmp/vibecomfy-runresult.json\"\n+REMOTE_SCRIPT = \"/tmp/vibecomfy-template-run.sh\"\n+DEFAULT_EXCLUDE = {\n+    \".git\",\n+    \".venv\",\n+    \"__pycache__\",\n+    \".pytest_cache\",\n+    \"vendor/direct_templates\",\n+    \"out/runs\",\n+    \"output\",\n+}\n+\n+\n+class RunPodTemplate:\n+    @classmethod\n+    @asynccontextmanager\n+    async def launch(\n+        cls,\n+        template_id: str,\n+        *,\n+        gpu: str | None = None,\n+        timeout: int = 1800,\n+        name_prefix: str = \"vibecomfy-template\",\n+        exclude: set[str] | None = None,\n+    ) -> AsyncIterator[RunResult]:\n+        guard = PodGuard(\n+            name_prefix=name_prefix,\n+            default_max_runtime_seconds=max(timeout * 2, 7200),\n+            gpu_type=gpu,\n+        )\n+        artifact_dir = _artifact_dir(template_id)\n+        artifact_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+        log_path = artifact_dir / \"run.log\"\n+        payload_path = artifact_dir / \"runresult.json\"\n+        pod = None\n+        start = time.monotonic()\n+        try:\n+            pod = await guard.launch()\n+            await pod.wait_ready(timeout=300)\n+            client = pod.open_ssh_client()\n+            try:\n+                sftp = client.open_sftp()\n+                try:\n+                    upload_dir(sftp, ROOT, REMOTE_ROOT, exclude or DEFAULT_EXCLUDE)\n+                    _upload_script(sftp, template_id)\n+                    code, stdout, stderr = await pod.exec_ssh(f\"bash {REMOTE_SCRIPT}\", timeout=timeout)\n+                    log_path.write_text(_format_log(code, stdout, stderr), encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+                    payload = _download_payload(sftp, payload_path)\n+                    _download_media(sftp, payload, artifact_dir)\n+                finally:\n+                    sftp.close()\n+            finally:\n+                client.close()\n+            yield RunResult.from_remote_payload(\n+                payload,\n+                artifact_dir=artifact_dir,\n+                logs=log_path,\n+                pod_id=getattr(pod, \"id\", \"\"),\n+                template_id=template_id,\n+            )\n+        except asyncio.TimeoutError as exc:\n+            elapsed = time.monotonic() - start\n+            log_path.write_text(f\"timeout after {timeout}s: {exc}\\n\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+            yield RunResult(\n+                status=\"timeout\",\n+                template_id=template_id,\n+                pod_id=getattr(pod, \"id\", \"\") if pod is not None else \"\",\n+                elapsed=elapsed,\n+                media_files=[],\n+                artifact_dir=artifact_dir,\n+                logs=log_path,\n+                error_message=str(exc) or f\"Timed out after {timeout} seconds.\",\n+            )\n+        finally:\n+            await guard.terminate()\n+\n+\n+def _artifact_dir(template_id: str) -> Path:\n+    stamp = datetime.now(UTC).strftime(\"%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ\")\n+    safe_template = template_id.replace(\"/\", \"_\")\n+    return ROOT / \"out\" / \"runpod_template\" / f\"{stamp}-{safe_template}\"\n+\n+\n+def _upload_script(sftp, template_id: str) -> None:\n+    remote_script = build_remote_script(\n+        template_id,\n+        remote_root=REMOTE_ROOT,\n+        payload_path=REMOTE_PAYLOAD,\n+        output_dir=\"output\",\n+    )\n+    handle = tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(prefix=\"vibecomfy-template-run-\", suffix=\".sh\", mode=\"w\", delete=False)\n+    script_path = Path(handle.name)\n+    try:\n+        handle.write(remote_script)\n+        handle.write(\"\\n\")\n+        handle.close()\n+        sftp.put(str(script_path), REMOTE_SCRIPT, confirm=False)\n+    finally:\n+        script_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n+\n+\n+def _download_payload(sftp, payload_path: Path) -> dict:\n+    try:\n+        sftp.get(REMOTE_PAYLOAD, str(payload_path))\n+    except OSError as exc:\n+        payload = {\n+            \"status\": \"error\",\n+            \"elapsed_seconds\": 0.0,\n+            \"media_files\": [],\n+            \"error_message\": f\"remote payload download failed: {exc}\",\n+        }\n+        payload_path.write_text(json.dumps(payload), encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+        return payload\n+    return json.loads(payload_path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n+\n+\n+def _download_media(sftp, payload: dict, artifact_dir: Path) -> None:\n+    local_media = []\n+    for entry in payload.get(\"media_files\", []):\n+        remote_path = str(entry)\n+        local_rel = _local_media_relative(remote_path)\n+        local_path = artifact_dir / local_rel\n+        local_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+        sftp.get(_remote_media_path(remote_path), str(local_path))\n+        local_media.append(local_rel.as_posix())\n+    payload[\"media_files\"] = local_media\n+\n+\n+def _remote_media_path(path: str) -> str:\n+    if path.startswith(\"/\"):\n+        return path\n+    return posixpath.join(REMOTE_ROOT, path)\n+\n+\n+def _local_media_relative(path: str) -> Path:\n+    if path.startswith(\"/\"):\n+        return Path(\"media\") / Path(path).name\n+    return Path(\"media\") / Path(path)\n+\n+\n+def _format_log(code: int, stdout: str, stderr: str) -> str:\n+    return f\"exit_code={code}\\n--- stdout ---\\n{stdout}\\n--- stderr ---\\n{stderr}\\n\"\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Idea:
Implement the smallest concrete build recommendation in docs/runpod_template_runner.md: lift the existing ad-hoc RunPod glue out of scripts/ into a clean importable vibecomfy.runpod package, so any caller (tests, CLI, future agents) can spin up a pod and run a ready template end-to-end with one async call.

This is a follow-up to the in-flight P1 megaplan (p1-foundation-typed-handle-20260426-1927). Zero file overlap with P1 — P1 owns vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, and tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. This megaplan owns vibecomfy/runpod/, scripts/runpod_runner.py (refactor), vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py (touch only if needed), and pyproject.toml.

DELIVERABLES — all must land:

1. New package vibecomfy/runpod/ with at least:
   - runner.py exposing RunPodTemplate with the async-context-manager API shown in the doc:
     async with RunPodTemplate.launch('image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i', gpu='RTX 4090') as result:
         assert result.status == 'ok'
         assert result.media_files
         # result.elapsed, result.logs, result.artifact_dir
   - result.py (or co-located) defining a RunResult dataclass with at minimum: status (Literal['ok','error','timeout']), media_files (list[Path]), elapsed (float seconds), logs (str or Path), artifact_dir (Path), pod_id (str), template_id (str). Add error_message field for non-ok statuses.
   - __init__.py exporting RunPodTemplate and RunResult.

2. Lift PodGuard and lifecycle-import helper out of scripts/runpod_runner.py (current locations: PodGuard at lines 27-82, _runpod_lifecycle() at lines 264-271) into vibecomfy/runpod/. Keep scripts/runpod_runner.py callers working via re-export — the script layer should import from the new package and re-expose the same names so existing callers in scripts/ and any external users of these scripts don't break.

3. Small remote script sent by RunPodTemplate.launch(...) that does: install vibecomfy on the pod → load template via vibecomfy.registry.ready.workflow_from_ready(template_id) → vibecomfy validate → vibecomfy run → emit a JSON result blob with media paths, timings, status, error message if any. NOT the 750-line bash blob from scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py:_remote_script — keep this one narrow (target: under 100 lines of remote bash/python). The RunPodTemplate launch flow parses that JSON into RunResult.

4. Declare runpod-lifecycle as a real dependency in pyproject.toml. Today it's discovered via sys.path / env-var hacks (VIBECOMFY_RUNPOD_LIFECYCLE_ROOT, sibling ../runpod-lifecycle checkout). Use [tool.uv.sources] with a path dep, OR optional-dependency, OR whichever pattern matches the project's current dependency management style — read pyproject.toml first to match conventions. Preserve the env-var fallback for now (don't break existing dev setups), but the canonical path becomes the declared dep.

5. One @pytest.mark.gpu test that exercises Flux Klein 4B T2I end-to-end against a live pod via RunPodTemplate.launch('image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i', gpu='RTX 4090'). Asserts result.status == 'ok', result.media_files non-empty, timings within reasonable bound. Marked opt-in — never runs in default pytest, only with pytest -m gpu or equivalent. DO NOT EXECUTE THIS TEST during the megaplan run — provisioning real GPU pods burns credentials/budget. The deliverable is the test file existing and being correctly marked; the user runs it post-merge.

CRITICAL CONSTRAINTS:

- Backward compatibility: existing scripts/runpod_runner.py callers (and scripts/runpod_validate.py, scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py if they import from it) must keep working. Re-export PodGuard, run_pod, run_pod_detached, _runpod_lifecycle from the script-level module so import paths stay valid.
- Do NOT touch P1 territory: vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. P1 megaplan is in flight.
- Do NOT execute the GPU test as part of this megaplan — only scaffold it.
- Do NOT rewrite the corpus matrix (scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py). The doc notes that's a future follow-up 'once that proves out.' Out of scope.

KEY FILES (read first):

- docs/runpod_template_runner.md — full survey + recommendation (the spec for this work).
- scripts/runpod_runner.py — PodGuard (27-82), run_pod (120-163), run_pod_detached (166-261), _runpod_lifecycle (264-271). Source for the lift.
- vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py (10-43) — existing CLI bridge using the same sibling/env-var trick.

- tests/test_ready_templates.py (27-33) — local validation pattern.
- scripts/runpod_validate.py (1-37) — minimal smoke runner pattern.

- pyproject.toml — declare runpod-lifecycle dep here.
- docs/runpod.md — existing operator doc; check if it needs a pointer to the new module.

- from vibecomfy.runpod import RunPodTemplate, RunResult succeeds in a clean Python session.
- RunPodTemplate.launch(template_id, gpu=...) returns an async context manager that yields a RunResult typed correctly.

  "meta_commentary": "Execution order is critical: T1\u2192T2\u2192T3 (lift) \u2192 T4 (re-export) \u2192 T5/T6 (data + remote script, independent) \u2192 T7 (runner) \u2192 T8 (__init__) \u2192 T9 (pyproject) \u2192 T10/T11/T12 (test scaffold + docs + cleanup test) \u2192 T13 (validation). T1, T3, T5, T6, T9 have no inter-dependencies and can be implemented in parallel if the executor wants speed.\n\nThree structural fixes from the v1\u2192v2 critique are load-bearing \u2014 do not regress them:\n\n1. **FLAG-001 (cycle):** the upload helpers MUST live in vibecomfy/runpod/upload.py, not stay in scripts/runpod_runner.py. If you let the runner import upload helpers from scripts/, package init creates a cycle (scripts \u2192 vibecomfy.runpod.pod_guard \u2192 re-enters package init while __init__.py is mid-execution). Step 14.1's grep audit is the enforcement mechanism.\n\n2. **FLAG-002 (remote install):** the remote pod's bash script installs `.[dev]` ONLY. It must NOT try to install `.[runpod-launch]` or `.[runpod-local]`. Why: runpod-lifecycle is declared via `[tool.uv.sources]` pointing at a sibling checkout that the pod upload doesn't include. Plain pip on the pod can't resolve `[tool.uv.sources]` and would crash before validation. The pod is the *target* of launches \u2014 it doesn't need to launch pods itself.\n\n3. **FLAG-003 (cleanup):** RunPodTemplate.launch MUST be `@asynccontextmanager`, not a class with `__aenter__`/`__aexit__`. Python skips `__aexit__` if `__aenter__` raises \u2014 that would leak live pods. The generator-based form puts try/finally directly around `yield`, so terminate() runs no matter where the failure happens. T12's monkeypatch test is the objective evidence.\n\nLazy lifecycle import is the fourth invariant: nothing in vibecomfy/runpod/ does a top-level `import runpod_lifecycle`. Only `_runpod_lifecycle()` body has it, only `PodGuard.launch()` calls it. This is what lets a clean .[dev]-only install successfully `from vibecomfy.runpod import RunPodTemplate, RunResult` \u2014 verified by Step 12.1 / SC8.\n\nFor T7 specifically: the existing scripts/runpod_runner.py:130-163 has the same try/finally cleanup pattern around run_pod's body \u2014 mirror that approach. Use the existing exclude-set conventions from scripts/runpod_runner.py (look at how run_pod calls upload_dir for the default ignore patterns).\n\nFor T6: keep the inline Python tight. Let it call `vibecomfy.registry.ready.workflow_from_ready(template_id)` then subprocess `python -m vibecomfy.cli run` (matches the already-exercised path in scripts/runpod_validate.py:22-26). Don't reimplement workflow execution inline \u2014 that's how you get back to a 750-line bash blob.\n\nFor T9: read pyproject.toml first to see the existing optional-dependencies layout. Follow whatever indentation/quote style is already in use. The `[tool.uv.sources]` block goes near the bottom (or wherever uv config conventions place it for this project).\n\nFor T13: the GPU test MUST NOT execute. `pytest --collect-only -m gpu` is collection only \u2014 safe. Real `pytest -m gpu` provisions a real pod and burns budget. If a validation step would require provisioning, skip it.\n\nP1 boundary is non-negotiable: do not touch vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, or tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. Scaffolding the GPU test under tests/gpu/ (not tests/smoke/) is part of this \u2014 different directory than P1's smoke tests.\n\nTwo open questions inherited from the plan that the executor can decide pragmatically:\n- vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py duplication: leave untouched (brief said 'touch only if needed').\n- runpod-launch naming: keep `runpod-launch` as the new extra (do not rename/delete runpod-local \u2014 preserves backward compat for any user who installs it today).\n\nBaseline tests not captured (test command was not approved for execution before plan finalization). Executor should run `pytest -q` early to capture pre-existing failures and distinguish them from regressions introduced by this work.",

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the proposed _coercion_log.py under vibecomfy/blocks/ is supporting infrastructure that both blocks and workflow need. given the current package dependency direction (blocks imports workflow), the safer location is a neutral module such as vibecomfy/handles.py or vibecomfy/handle_warnings.py; leaving it under blocks increases import-order fragility unless the implementation is very deliberate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handle-equality-hash-semantics: strict python hash/eq invariant violated for the bare-id compatibility bridge (handle('1', 0) == '1' and == '1.0' but hash('1') != hash('1.0')). (flagged 2 times across 2 plans)

  "meta_commentary": "Execution order is critical: T1\u2192T2\u2192T3 (lift) \u2192 T4 (re-export) \u2192 T5/T6 (data + remote script, independent) \u2192 T7 (runner) \u2192 T8 (__init__) \u2192 T9 (pyproject) \u2192 T10/T11/T12 (test scaffold + docs + cleanup test) \u2192 T13 (validation). T1, T3, T5, T6, T9 have no inter-dependencies and can be implemented in parallel if the executor wants speed.\n\nThree structural fixes from the v1\u2192v2 critique are load-bearing \u2014 do not regress them:\n\n1. **FLAG-001 (cycle):** the upload helpers MUST live in vibecomfy/runpod/upload.py, not stay in scripts/runpod_runner.py. If you let the runner import upload helpers from scripts/, package init creates a cycle (scripts \u2192 vibecomfy.runpod.pod_guard \u2192 re-enters package init while __init__.py is mid-execution). Step 14.1's grep audit is the enforcement mechanism.\n\n2. **FLAG-002 (remote install):** the remote pod's bash script installs `.[dev]` ONLY. It must NOT try to install `.[runpod-launch]` or `.[runpod-local]`. Why: runpod-lifecycle is declared via `[tool.uv.sources]` pointing at a sibling checkout that the pod upload doesn't include. Plain pip on the pod can't resolve `[tool.uv.sources]` and would crash before validation. The pod is the *target* of launches \u2014 it doesn't need to launch pods itself.\n\n3. **FLAG-003 (cleanup):** RunPodTemplate.launch MUST be `@asynccontextmanager`, not a class with `__aenter__`/`__aexit__`. Python skips `__aexit__` if `__aenter__` raises \u2014 that would leak live pods. The generator-based form puts try/finally directly around `yield`, so terminate() runs no matter where the failure happens. T12's monkeypatch test is the objective evidence.\n\nLazy lifecycle import is the fourth invariant: nothing in vibecomfy/runpod/ does a top-level `import runpod_lifecycle`. Only `_runpod_lifecycle()` body has it, only `PodGuard.launch()` calls it. This is what lets a clean .[dev]-only install successfully `from vibecomfy.runpod import RunPodTemplate, RunResult` \u2014 verified by Step 12.1 / SC8.\n\nFor T7 specifically: the existing scripts/runpod_runner.py:130-163 has the same try/finally cleanup pattern around run_pod's body \u2014 mirror that approach. Use the existing exclude-set conventions from scripts/runpod_runner.py (look at how run_pod calls upload_dir for the default ignore patterns).\n\nFor T6: keep the inline Python tight. Let it call `vibecomfy.registry.ready.workflow_from_ready(template_id)` then subprocess `python -m vibecomfy.cli run` (matches the already-exercised path in scripts/runpod_validate.py:22-26). Don't reimplement workflow execution inline \u2014 that's how you get back to a 750-line bash blob.\n\nFor T9: read pyproject.toml first to see the existing optional-dependencies layout. Follow whatever indentation/quote style is already in use. The `[tool.uv.sources]` block goes near the bottom (or wherever uv config conventions place it for this project).\n\nFor T13: the GPU test MUST NOT execute. `pytest --collect-only -m gpu` is collection only \u2014 safe. Real `pytest -m gpu` provisions a real pod and burns budget. If a validation step would require provisioning, skip it.\n\nP1 boundary is non-negotiable: do not touch vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, or tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. Scaffolding the GPU test under tests/gpu/ (not tests/smoke/) is part of this \u2014 different directory than P1's smoke tests.\n\nTwo open questions inherited from the plan that the executor can decide pragmatically:\n- vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py duplication: leave untouched (brief said 'touch only if needed').\n- runpod-launch naming: keep `runpod-launch` as the new extra (do not rename/delete runpod-local \u2014 preserves backward compat for any user who installs it today).\n\nBaseline tests not captured (test command was not approved for execution before plan finalization). Executor should run `pytest -q` early to capture pre-existing failures and distinguish them from regressions introduced by this work.",

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Project directory:

        Idea:
Implement the smallest concrete build recommendation in docs/runpod_template_runner.md: lift the existing ad-hoc RunPod glue out of scripts/ into a clean importable vibecomfy.runpod package, so any caller (tests, CLI, future agents) can spin up a pod and run a ready template end-to-end with one async call.

This is a follow-up to the in-flight P1 megaplan (p1-foundation-typed-handle-20260426-1927). Zero file overlap with P1 — P1 owns vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, and tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. This megaplan owns vibecomfy/runpod/, scripts/runpod_runner.py (refactor), vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py (touch only if needed), and pyproject.toml.

DELIVERABLES — all must land:

1. New package vibecomfy/runpod/ with at least:
   - runner.py exposing RunPodTemplate with the async-context-manager API shown in the doc:
     async with RunPodTemplate.launch('image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i', gpu='RTX 4090') as result:
         assert result.status == 'ok'
         assert result.media_files
         # result.elapsed, result.logs, result.artifact_dir
   - result.py (or co-located) defining a RunResult dataclass with at minimum: status (Literal['ok','error','timeout']), media_files (list[Path]), elapsed (float seconds), logs (str or Path), artifact_dir (Path), pod_id (str), template_id (str). Add error_message field for non-ok statuses.
   - __init__.py exporting RunPodTemplate and RunResult.

2. Lift PodGuard and lifecycle-import helper out of scripts/runpod_runner.py (current locations: PodGuard at lines 27-82, _runpod_lifecycle() at lines 264-271) into vibecomfy/runpod/. Keep scripts/runpod_runner.py callers working via re-export — the script layer should import from the new package and re-expose the same names so existing callers in scripts/ and any external users of these scripts don't break.

3. Small remote script sent by RunPodTemplate.launch(...) that does: install vibecomfy on the pod → load template via vibecomfy.registry.ready.workflow_from_ready(template_id) → vibecomfy validate → vibecomfy run → emit a JSON result blob with media paths, timings, status, error message if any. NOT the 750-line bash blob from scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py:_remote_script — keep this one narrow (target: under 100 lines of remote bash/python). The RunPodTemplate launch flow parses that JSON into RunResult.

4. Declare runpod-lifecycle as a real dependency in pyproject.toml. Today it's discovered via sys.path / env-var hacks (VIBECOMFY_RUNPOD_LIFECYCLE_ROOT, sibling ../runpod-lifecycle checkout). Use [tool.uv.sources] with a path dep, OR optional-dependency, OR whichever pattern matches the project's current dependency management style — read pyproject.toml first to match conventions. Preserve the env-var fallback for now (don't break existing dev setups), but the canonical path becomes the declared dep.

5. One @pytest.mark.gpu test that exercises Flux Klein 4B T2I end-to-end against a live pod via RunPodTemplate.launch('image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i', gpu='RTX 4090'). Asserts result.status == 'ok', result.media_files non-empty, timings within reasonable bound. Marked opt-in — never runs in default pytest, only with pytest -m gpu or equivalent. DO NOT EXECUTE THIS TEST during the megaplan run — provisioning real GPU pods burns credentials/budget. The deliverable is the test file existing and being correctly marked; the user runs it post-merge.

CRITICAL CONSTRAINTS:

- Backward compatibility: existing scripts/runpod_runner.py callers (and scripts/runpod_validate.py, scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py if they import from it) must keep working. Re-export PodGuard, run_pod, run_pod_detached, _runpod_lifecycle from the script-level module so import paths stay valid.
- Do NOT touch P1 territory: vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. P1 megaplan is in flight.
- Do NOT execute the GPU test as part of this megaplan — only scaffold it.
- Do NOT rewrite the corpus matrix (scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py). The doc notes that's a future follow-up 'once that proves out.' Out of scope.

KEY FILES (read first):

- docs/runpod_template_runner.md — full survey + recommendation (the spec for this work).
- scripts/runpod_runner.py — PodGuard (27-82), run_pod (120-163), run_pod_detached (166-261), _runpod_lifecycle (264-271). Source for the lift.
- vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py (10-43) — existing CLI bridge using the same sibling/env-var trick.
- vibecomfy/registry/ready.py — ready_template_ids(), workflow_from_ready().
- tests/test_ready_templates.py (27-33) — local validation pattern.
- scripts/runpod_validate.py (1-37) — minimal smoke runner pattern.
- scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py:_remote_script (43-766) — the 750-line bash blob the new wrapper must NOT recreate; reference for what the 'minimal' version needs to do.
- pyproject.toml — declare runpod-lifecycle dep here.
- docs/runpod.md — existing operator doc; check if it needs a pointer to the new module.

ACCEPTANCE:

- All existing tests pass unchanged.
- from vibecomfy.runpod import RunPodTemplate, RunResult succeeds in a clean Python session.
- RunPodTemplate.launch(template_id, gpu=...) returns an async context manager that yields a RunResult typed correctly.
- scripts/runpod_runner.py imports continue to work via re-export.
- runpod-lifecycle is declared in pyproject.toml.
- pytest --collect-only -m gpu lists the new Flux Klein 4B T2I test.
- The new wrapper's remote script is documented (docstring or short doc note) and is materially shorter than the corpus-matrix bash blob.

    "FLAG-001 (circular imports): nothing in vibecomfy/runpod/ may import from scripts.* \u2014 Step 14.1 grep audit (`grep -RIn 'from scripts' vibecomfy/runpod/`) must return empty. The dependency direction is strictly one-way: scripts/runpod_runner.py imports from vibecomfy.runpod.*, never the reverse.",
    "FLAG-002 (remote install conflict): the remote bash script must `pip install -e '.[dev]'` only \u2014 NOT '.[runpod-launch]' and NOT '.[runpod-local]'. The pod is the target of launches and never needs runpod-lifecycle. Grep the rendered build_remote_script output to enforce.",
    "FLAG-003 (pod cleanup): RunPodTemplate.launch MUST use @asynccontextmanager (not a hand-written __aenter__/__aexit__ class) so the try/finally wraps the yield. T12's cleanup unit test is the objective verification \u2014 must show terminate awaited exactly once when wait_ready raises.",
    "Lazy lifecycle import: nothing in vibecomfy/runpod/ may do a top-level `import runpod_lifecycle`. The import only happens inside _runpod_lifecycle() body, called only by PodGuard.launch(). Verified by clean .[dev]-only install successfully importing vibecomfy.runpod.",
    "P1 territory is strictly untouched: vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. P1 megaplan (p1-foundation-typed-handle-20260426-1927) is in flight \u2014 file overlap will cause merge conflicts and break that megaplan.",
    "Backward compatibility: nine names must keep resolving from scripts.runpod_runner \u2014 PodGuard, run_pod, run_pod_detached, _runpod_lifecycle, runpod_lifecycle_root, should_skip, upload_dir, REMOTE_ROOT, ROOT. Validation step 13.2 covers this.",
    "DO NOT execute `pytest -m gpu` \u2014 provisioning real RTX 4090 pods burns RunPod credentials and budget. Only collect (--collect-only). The user runs it post-merge.",
    "DO NOT rewrite scripts/runpod_corpus_matrix.py \u2014 that's explicit out-of-scope future follow-up. Do not touch its 750-line _remote_script.",
    "vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py:10-43 has its own duplicated lifecycle-discovery helper \u2014 leave untouched per brief's 'touch only if needed'. The duplication is harmless.",

    "PodGuard.gpu_type kwarg must NOT mutate os.environ \u2014 it overrides VIBECOMFY_RUNPOD_GPU only inside that instance's launch() call. Mutating env would leak between concurrent launches.",

    "Existing pyproject.toml uses hatchling+PEP 621 with optional-dependencies (`dev`, `runpod-local`); no [tool.uv.sources] block today. Read the file first, then add the new sections without disturbing existing tables."

  "meta_commentary": "Execution order is critical: T1\u2192T2\u2192T3 (lift) \u2192 T4 (re-export) \u2192 T5/T6 (data + remote script, independent) \u2192 T7 (runner) \u2192 T8 (__init__) \u2192 T9 (pyproject) \u2192 T10/T11/T12 (test scaffold + docs + cleanup test) \u2192 T13 (validation). T1, T3, T5, T6, T9 have no inter-dependencies and can be implemented in parallel if the executor wants speed.\n\nThree structural fixes from the v1\u2192v2 critique are load-bearing \u2014 do not regress them:\n\n1. **FLAG-001 (cycle):** the upload helpers MUST live in vibecomfy/runpod/upload.py, not stay in scripts/runpod_runner.py. If you let the runner import upload helpers from scripts/, package init creates a cycle (scripts \u2192 vibecomfy.runpod.pod_guard \u2192 re-enters package init while __init__.py is mid-execution). Step 14.1's grep audit is the enforcement mechanism.\n\n2. **FLAG-002 (remote install):** the remote pod's bash script installs `.[dev]` ONLY. It must NOT try to install `.[runpod-launch]` or `.[runpod-local]`. Why: runpod-lifecycle is declared via `[tool.uv.sources]` pointing at a sibling checkout that the pod upload doesn't include. Plain pip on the pod can't resolve `[tool.uv.sources]` and would crash before validation. The pod is the *target* of launches \u2014 it doesn't need to launch pods itself.\n\n3. **FLAG-003 (cleanup):** RunPodTemplate.launch MUST be `@asynccontextmanager`, not a class with `__aenter__`/`__aexit__`. Python skips `__aexit__` if `__aenter__` raises \u2014 that would leak live pods. The generator-based form puts try/finally directly around `yield`, so terminate() runs no matter where the failure happens. T12's monkeypatch test is the objective evidence.\n\nLazy lifecycle import is the fourth invariant: nothing in vibecomfy/runpod/ does a top-level `import runpod_lifecycle`. Only `_runpod_lifecycle()` body has it, only `PodGuard.launch()` calls it. This is what lets a clean .[dev]-only install successfully `from vibecomfy.runpod import RunPodTemplate, RunResult` \u2014 verified by Step 12.1 / SC8.\n\nFor T7 specifically: the existing scripts/runpod_runner.py:130-163 has the same try/finally cleanup pattern around run_pod's body \u2014 mirror that approach. Use the existing exclude-set conventions from scripts/runpod_runner.py (look at how run_pod calls upload_dir for the default ignore patterns).\n\nFor T6: keep the inline Python tight. Let it call `vibecomfy.registry.ready.workflow_from_ready(template_id)` then subprocess `python -m vibecomfy.cli run` (matches the already-exercised path in scripts/runpod_validate.py:22-26). Don't reimplement workflow execution inline \u2014 that's how you get back to a 750-line bash blob.\n\nFor T9: read pyproject.toml first to see the existing optional-dependencies layout. Follow whatever indentation/quote style is already in use. The `[tool.uv.sources]` block goes near the bottom (or wherever uv config conventions place it for this project).\n\nFor T13: the GPU test MUST NOT execute. `pytest --collect-only -m gpu` is collection only \u2014 safe. Real `pytest -m gpu` provisions a real pod and burns budget. If a validation step would require provisioning, skip it.\n\nP1 boundary is non-negotiable: do not touch vibecomfy/blocks/, vibecomfy/workflow.py, vibecomfy/runtime/session.py, or tests/smoke/test_p1_runpod.py. Scaffolding the GPU test under tests/gpu/ (not tests/smoke/) is part of this \u2014 different directory than P1's smoke tests.\n\nTwo open questions inherited from the plan that the executor can decide pragmatically:\n- vibecomfy/commands/runpod.py duplication: leave untouched (brief said 'touch only if needed').\n- runpod-launch naming: keep `runpod-launch` as the new extra (do not rename/delete runpod-local \u2014 preserves backward compat for any user who installs it today).\n\nBaseline tests not captured (test command was not approved for execution before plan finalization). Executor should run `pytest -q` early to capture pre-existing failures and distinguish them from regressions introduced by this work.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan places _coercion_log.py under vibecomfy/blocks/ but also needs vibeworkflow.connect in vibecomfy/workflow.py to consult handle_trace_active and record events. because importing vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log executes vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py, and that package currently imports vibeworkflow at top level, any top-level import from workflow.py to vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log would create a circular import risk unless the implementation uses carefully lazy imports or moves the shared trace module outside the blocks package. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure: does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the proposed _coercion_log.py under vibecomfy/blocks/ is supporting infrastructure that both blocks and workflow need. given the current package dependency direction (blocks imports workflow), the safer location is a neutral module such as vibecomfy/handles.py or vibecomfy/handle_warnings.py; leaving it under blocks increases import-order fragility unless the implementation is very deliberate. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them: find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: for the block helper caller path, vibeworkflow.connect is usually called by vibecomfy.blocks._utils.connect, not by the user's block function directly. the plan's immediate-caller-frame heuristic can work if _utils.connect has a handle local named source, but it will not identify the higher-level block function unless record_raw_ref_coercion preserves or walks to the block frame rather than skipping blocks frames. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] handle-equality-hash-semantics: strict python hash/eq invariant violated for the bare-id compatibility bridge (handle('1', 0) == '1' and == '1.0' but hash('1') != hash('1.0')). (flagged 2 times across 2 plans)
- [DEBT] handle-equality-hash-semantics: hash/eq compromise is documented but not strictly satisfied for bare-id bridge. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] import-architecture: import architecture: placing shared trace state in `vibecomfy.blocks._coercion_log` risks circular imports because `workflow.py` needs the state for connect-site detection while `vibecomfy.blocks.__init__` currently imports `vibeworkflow` at package import time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader: search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the plan's record_raw_ref_coercion helper says it finds the first non-vibecomfy/blocks frame for the site. that conflicts with the stated goal that raw string refs in block code are visible: a missed handles(image="...") inside a block called by a ready template could be attributed to an allowlisted ready_templates frame and suppressed, or at minimum would not identify the block module as the source. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] untyped-raw-ref-doctor-rule-coverage: runtime detection covers handles values and connect refs but not arbitrary python dict keys outside those flows. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] wf-node-out-api-contract: non-digit slot names raise notimplementederror instead of returning a handle; diverges from brief's .out(name_or_index) literal wording. (flagged 2 times across 2 plans)
- [DEBT] wf-node-out-api-contract: same as issue_hints-1. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
